The reason is the pytest-node manifest: rows appended and retired inside this slice are illegal from any base that predates them, and removing them renumbers, which the repository's successor guard refuses as a repoint. That guard's own waiver admits exactly one regeneration — legal against the landing base, removing nothing outside the rows that base never had, with the appended block re-derived in collection order — and a regeneration meeting it is legal from the landing base alone, because the step-0 commit's own two appended rows are re-ordered with the rest. Recorded as a roster amendment, not a re-scope: no gate left the roster and none was added |
| 5 | Terminal correction loop | only via a recorded roster-addition checkpoint | that batch |

### Owner decision recorded at step 0 — both child entry forms

The implementation plan's D3 asked whether a child invoked by a terminal `process_call` receives
its caller's documents, because every canonical root emits a scheduled No Data Start. Verified
against official Boomi documentation (the Process Call step page): for a non-passthrough subprocess
"Documents are not passed into the subprocess" and "The parent process calls a new instance of the
subprocess for each document that reaches the Process Call step"; a passthrough subprocess receives
"any documents that reach the Process Call step as a group" in "one instance of the subprocess".
The #156 archive agrees: its No Data recovery child got its own execution record, one inbound
document per invocation.

The question was put to the owner. Decision, verbatim: "Well, we need to be able to support both
cases - most use of subprocesses use pass through but depending on logic it could be no data and in
this case we see the sub process as a separate in log, which could be useful for some cases, check
docs on this topic and apply what should we actually have for this case." #184 therefore supports a
Data Passthrough child and a No Data child, each with its documented semantics; the design is the
attested amendments 1 and 2.

### Owner decision recorded 2026-09-14 — an unreadable account degrades the typed plan

Asked during Stage-2 correction batch 12, after QA-184-s1-r11-01 and QA-184-s1-r12-01. The question was "Should build_integration plan/compile still work when the Boomi account can't be read? Fail-closed keeps breaking the clean-room suite, which compiles without a real account, and fixing its fixtures means a real rewrite." The options were to degrade honestly, to block and rebuild the clean-room fixtures, or to block and have the clean-room tests expect the refusal. The owner answered: "Degrade honestly (Recommended)".

What it decides:
- When a client is in hand and the component plan cannot be built, plan and compile keep working. The served advisory names every verdict the component plan decides and says apply refuses a binding the account decides differently.
- Apply's recompute against the readable account is the guard for a binding compiled while the account was unreadable.
- The fail-closed block of correction batch 11 is withdrawn, and the clean-room suite and its fixtures are unchanged.

### Data Passthrough Start provenance source (pre-baseline, platform-authored)

`tests/fixtures/live_xml/m11/` holds four live captures committed at `e512c24` (2026-07-02, #119 census),
long before this slice's baseline, whose Start is `<passthroughaction/>`:
`process_setproperties_ddp_dpp_current_crossref.xml`, `process_cache_branch_load_remove.xml`,
`process_dpp_profile_decision_flow.xml`, `process_doccacheretrieve_loadalldoc_variant.xml`. Their
`<process>` open tag reads `allowSimultaneous="false" enableUserLog="false" processLogOnErrorOnly="false"
purgeDataImmediately="false" updateRunDates="false" workload="general"` (measured on two of the four),
with no `stopProcessingIfZeroDocuments` attribute. Provenance class: **live capture of a
platform-stored process**, causally independent of the implementation under test. Whether the
platform DELIVERS a caller's documents to such a child is not in these captures and is owed to
live evidence (`e0`) and Stage-1 QA.

| C1 | the design plan: the universal property-profile proof conflicts with active survivor `golden-000072`, which blocks completion until an initial-document authority or an acceptance-policy amendment exists | resolved by amendment 2 without a policy amendment: under the documented No Data semantics the survivor's first writer reads a profile element from the single empty start document, so its bound GET path gets an empty segment and addresses a different resource. | C2 | the implementation plan D1: a profile writer on the untouched entry stream records an entry requirement, keeping `golden-000072` unchanged | superseded by amendment 1's entry model: a scheduled root's entry is the single EMPTY document (a profile read there is refused, C1); only a Data Passthrough root's entry carries caller documents, and only there does an entry-position profile consumer define a requirement a caller discharges | amendment 1 §2; the Process Call and Start step pages |

| C4 | amendment 1: the catch-body non-notify prefix refusal keeps its `/steps/<offender>` pointer (implementation plan D9) | A6 requires the residual prefix refusal at `/…/terminal`; amendment 1 adopts `/…/terminal` and this ledger follows A6 | issue #184 A6 text |
| C5 | the implementation plan D2 consumer table: a call fed by a cache read is checked when it declares an input, at `/operation_ref` | not implemented. A cache's content may itself be a call's output, so comparing it with the next call's input is call-to-call profile equality, which D4 leaves unchecked because connector request and response profiles are documented as non-validating. A wrong map ahead of the write is already refused at the map, and a write that contradicts the cache's declared profile at `/cache_ref`. Only a map-origin stream is compared with the call it feeds, at the map's own `/map_ref` (#140's pointer) | the D4 rationale in `connector_resolution.py` (`validate_connector_call_semantics`); the connector-call branch of the stream step in `semantic_validation/lineage.py` |
| C6 | the implementation plan D2: the legacy flag means a legacy `source` is upstream on the path, and it is sticky | scoped to the dialect. A consumer is exempt only while the documents reaching it were last produced by a legacy `source` (a first-class call clears the flag, as the old walk re-checked a bracketed map after a call even under a source root), or when its subtree ends only at a legacy `target` with no connector call. Measured: goldens 000022/040/041/078 are the adapter spine `[listener, map_ref, target, stop]` with symbols that declare no profile at all, so their maps were unchecked since #158 and nothing about them is provable. The plan named this exact case as possible owner input "if adapter symbols lack map profiles"; they lack every profile and sit on a legacy `target`, which is the genuine legacy dialect the design plan exempts by name. A document-scoped flag was rejected on measurement: the model admits a connector-call leg beside a legacy-target leg under a control-only root, so it would have exempted the call leg's maps | scratch capture of the adapter IR and symbols for the four goldens; the mixing probe (`leg_target_under_root_call` and `leg_call_then_target_under_control_root` refused `PROCESS_IR_SCHEMA_INVALID_CARDINALITY`, sibling call and target legs admitted); `tests/test_issue_184_stream_profiles.py` legacy-scoping tests |
| C7 | the implementation plan D2: a Message or Data Process after the entry leaves an entry stream that is no longer pristine | kept as a separate "touched entry" state with no producer: a map or cache write there stays a cardinality refusal owned by connector resolution (A4), with no second profile finding beside it; a profile-valued source there is not checked, because a Message may build a document of that profile; a profile-valued source on the untouched empty entry is refused (amendment 2 §4). Document existence and stream profile therefore agree: exactly the no-producer points of the connector walk are the three no-producer stream states | `semantic_validation/lineage.py` stream states; `tests/test_issue_184_absent_stream.py` (`message_is_not_a_from_nothing_producer`) |
| C8 | the implementation plan D11: keep #141's pending-map obligation inside try and catch bodies as an error-context rule in the lineage controller | kept in connector resolution instead, verbatim, restricted by a per-path flag set when the walk passes a `try_catch`. The rule exists there already; restating it in the lineage controller would be a second copy of one rule. Everywhere else the bracketing half is removed and the stream-profile proof owns maps | `connector_resolution._PathState.error_context`; the existing error-handling and notify-recovery suites pass unchanged |
| C9 | none of the plans | the lineage controller now raises #140's profile code at maps, cache writes and property sources that connector resolution never visits, so the compile route had no delegated original to restore its static text from and would have served the generic fallback. The compile gate now rebuilds a code's static compiler text by code when no delegated original exists and the code has no validation-table text | `compiler/process_ir/pipeline.py` `_enforce_semantic_report` |
| C10 | the implementation plan D12's non-vacuity witness: run the old recursive walker from a `git archive` of the step-0 baseline against the new controller, over every committed IR fixture plus long generated cases | anchored at the stream-profile commit `2758e03` instead. The step-0 walker predates this slice's intended lineage changes (A5 invalidation, the stream-profile proof), so its findings differ by design; only the last recursive walker isolates the refactor. Scope: all 40 ProcessIR documents under `tests/fixtures` × 4 corpus symbol tables, plus two generated 300-step cases, = 160 walks. Result: 152 identical in findings, required reads and guaranteed writes; the 8 walks the old walker truncated (the generated cases) all keep every old finding in the new walk; 0 mismatches. A first run scanned only `tests/fixtures/process_ir` (27 documents) and was widened after the count was measured. Withdrawn along with the bound: the walk's `truncated` field, the `walk_truncated` inert reason, and its served clause. A non-tree graph now raises `PROCESS_IR_COMPILE_INTERNAL` | `docs/architecture/evidence/issue-184/sweeps/d12-controller/`; `tests/test_issue_184_lineage_controller.py` |
| C11 | C7 above: "exactly the no-producer points of the connector walk are the three no-producer stream states" | amendment 3 §3 separates two facts that C7's wording merged. The first is whether a document ARRIVES to trigger a step: a scheduled Start supplies one empty trigger document, and a cache load or an all-document remove exhausts its path. The second is whether the arriving payload is USABLE by a map or a cache write. A4's refusals on the empty entry are the compiler's usable-payload requirement; they are not evidence that a No Data Start emits zero documents. A cache read replaces the stream only when a document triggers it, and it cannot restart an exhausted one. A declared non-producing call followed by a cache read is refused at the call's `/operation_ref`, and `cap184-send-then-read` stays OPEN | rows E0-184-01, E0-184-03, E1-184-01; `agents/reports/2026-09-13-issue-184-evidence-e1.md`; amendment 3 §1, §3 |
| C12 | the design plan's A5 and this slice's A5 increment `703ba44`: a cache read loses every document-scoped property (the survival table's "lost" cell), extended to ordinary reads | contradicted by measurement. With exactly one current and one cached document, a triggered retrieve carries the cached document's DDPs overlaid on the current document's. On collision the cached value wins (R1), a current-only name survives (R2, R4), and the cached value alone is kept (R3). N current × M cached is OPEN. Amendment 3 §7 replaces invalidation with a bounded overlay over frozen cache cohorts with writer alternatives. The A5 probe's linear `cache_put → cache_get` graph never executed its read, so it is refused for its dead successor, and a staged equivalent (terminal write leg, separately triggered read leg) becomes the positive witness | row E0-184-02; `docs/architecture/evidence/issue-184/captures/cap184-retrieve-ddp-replacement/`; amendment 3 §7 |
| C13 | the branch point's cache adjacency rules: the model requires a cache read straight after a cache write (`_check_cache_put_followed_by_read`), tolerates a Stop or Exception after a trailing cache write (`_check_trailing_cache_put` and its terminal set), and the emitter registry gives cache load and remove a continuing successor. The design plan's matrix row `[call, cache_put, cache_get, stop]` is admitted | every one of these authorizes a dead edge: the platform skips the successor and reads COMPLETE. They are replaced by one terminal cache-action verdict derived from a document-emission authority. An authored successor of `cache_put` or `cache_remove` is refused `PROCESS_IR_SCHEMA_INVALID_CARDINALITY` at the cache node's `/cache_ref`. `cache_put` stays terminal in its Branch-leg and catch slots, and `cache_remove` gains a Branch-leg terminal only. The emitter registry gives load and remove exactly zero outgoing edges, and the renderer and graph verifier refuse an outbound wire. On the legacy route, the inline remove transform, flow-sequence cache successors and a DLQ cache with an authored Exception are refused, and the synthetic Stop after terminal DLQ staging is omitted | amendment 3 §2, §4, §5; the active-golden scan in C15 |
| C14 | amendment 2: the DDP hand-off into a passthrough child stays fail-closed until a capture attests it; amendment 1's prefix rows are keyed by context and direct predecessor only | `cap184-passthrough-ddp-handoff` discharged the gate for a waited passthrough child: the child, which has no Set Properties step, bound three inherited paths that matched their own payloads. Prefix admission is keyed by (context, direct predecessor, child entry form, wait), and only passthrough with wait=true is admitted. The per-document No Data capture attests no prefix, so No Data prefixes stay refused `PROCESS_IR_CAPABILITY_PROCESS_CALL_PLACEMENT_UNSUPPORTED` at `/…/terminal`. Passthrough wait=false stays refused under a new entry-context capability code at `/…/wait`. Child contracts are derived without requiring effect declarations and discharged per invocation. Direct execution of a passthrough process runs as No Data, so a called contract does not establish standalone validity | `agents/reports/2026-09-13-issue-184-evidence-e0.md`; amendment 3 §8 |

The exit meet counts a terminal call as a completion, so a derived must-set no longer over-claims. The cached document properties and their writer alternatives cross the boundary too (amendment 3 §7–§8), proved at each call against every cohort that reaches it | correction batch 18 |

## Defect-class ledger (a class is a (mechanism, runtime-authority) pair, assigned at reconciliation)

| Class | Mechanism | Runtime authority | Instances (derived from rows) | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `cache-step-document-emission` | a hand-written rule for which steps hand documents to their successor: cache writes and removes are modelled as consuming the stream but restarted by an immediately following cache read, or as passing documents on (the emitter registry admits a successor after a cache load or remove; the model requires a cache read straight after a cache write) | platform execution: a step with no inbound documents is skipped ("No documents found. Skipping execution for …") while the execution still reads COMPLETE | E0-184-01, E0-184-03, E1-184-01 — **third instance**; QA-184-s1-r14-01 — **recurred after the structural fix**; ARCH-184-r1-08 — **recurred again** | structural fix mandatory in-slice: one measured document-emission authority replacing the enumerations, with a sibling sweep, a non-vacuity witness and a coverage claim. Planned by amendment 3 §1–§6 (C11, C13, C15); the confirmation captures are archived; the sibling inventory is recorded under this table, completed by correction batch 14 (SELF-184-29, SELF-184-32, SELF-184-33, SELF-184-34). **Fourth instance (QA round r14, QA-184-s1-r14-01):** the served placement rows projected the type-admission matrix, which admits both sink kinds in every step slot, instead of the authority. That is the structural fix's served-inventory sibling, which the sweep had checked only through the ordering sentence. Materially different action in the same batch: the served rows are derived from the authority and pinned to the placements that compile (`test_served_placements_are_the_placements_that_compile`), so a served placement can no longer disagree with the compile verdict **Fifth instance (architect evaluation 1, ARCH-184-r1-08):** the sibling inventory's rendering row accepted the registry's and the legacy adapter's caller-side guards for the two sink renderers, where amendment 3 §4 requires the renderers to refuse a wire themselves. Materially different action delivered in correction batch 18: the renderers read the authority's zero-emission kinds themselves, and a two-way pin over the rendering module derives its sibling set from the authority. |
| `exception-parameter-source-unrendered` | an accepted authoring value (`parameter_source` of `none`) that the renderer turns into an Exception with no parameter block | the platform's component schema, which requires one on create | E1-184-02 | first instance |
| `confounded-measured-cell` | a measured table cell whose capture could not exercise it (the r17 cache rows wired a cache load straight into the retrieve, so the retrieve never ran and "lost" was read off a skipped step) | platform execution | E0-184-02 | first instance; re-measure with discriminating captures, then re-derive the cell |
| `child-contract-omits-cache-content` | a hand-picked subset of a child's stream facts carried in its entry contract: its document-profile and writer requirements were carried, but not what its consumers need of a cache it reads first. So a caller's writes discharged the child's cache read without proving the profile its map consumes | the lineage stream-profile proof: content facts on the cache key, and the consumers that check them | QA-184-s1-r1-02, ARCH-184-r1-05 — **second instance**, ARCH-184-r1-06 — **third instance** (revised authority) | first instance; one recorder (`_requires`) now feeds both requirement channels from every consumer site, so the contract carries every requirement the proof collects **Revision (reconciliation of architect evaluation 1; original retained above):** ARCH-184-r1-05 is the same mechanism for the cached document properties and their writer alternatives. ARCH-184-r1-06 leaves out the guaranteed execution state and approximates rule 8 by a set overlap: again a hand-picked subset of the child's walk carried in its contract. The authority is revised to the one both findings read: the child's own lineage walk at its boundary, every component of its state (document, execution, cache content and property cohorts) on the requirement side and on the possible and guaranteed effect sides. The recorded authority named one component of that walk; the walk, the derivation (`derive_child_entry_facts`) and the per-call discharge are one subsystem, so this reads the same authority whole rather than a different one, and no follow-up is owed. Second instance, so the structural fix landed in correction batch 18: the contract projects the child's walk over every state component, pinned both ways by a component × side coverage table with justified cells, with a sibling sweep and a non-vacuity witness. |
| `served-rule-absent-from-node-page` | a rule served on one node page (`node.passthrough`) and missing from the page a caller reads for the construct it constrains (`node.process_call`) | the served authoring-contract projection | QA-184-s1-r1-01 | first instance |
| `cache-identity-by-authored-ref` | cache facts keyed by the authored ref, with alias handling patched at individual sites (`_respelled` in the child derivation, `_cache_identity` in the child cache-write marking) | the symbol table's resolved documentcache component id | CDX-184-r1-01, QA-184-s1-r3-01, CDX-184-r2-02, SELF-184-04, CDX-184-r3-01, CDX-184-r4-01, CDX-184-r4-02, CDX-184-r4-03, CDX-184-r5-01, CDX-184-r5-02, SELF-184-08, QA-184-s1-r7-02, CDX-184-r7-01, QA-184-s1-r9-01, CDX-184-r8-01, CDX-184-r8-02, CDX-184-r8-03 — **recurred after the structural fix** | structural on the first reported instance, because two per-site alias patches already existed. One canonicalization derived from the symbol table is applied at every validation entry, to the lowered graph copy and to the trusted context, and both per-site patches are deleted. Sibling sweep: every cache-keyed fact passes through it — establishment keys from reads and writes, cache content, property cohorts, whole-cache removal, external-writer rows, child-contract and seed cache refs, and effect read/write keys. Witness: `test_the_canonical_cache_spelling_is_load_bearing`. **Revision (correction batch 3; original retained above):** that fix derived identity from the symbol's `component_id`, which a plan-time symbol table sets to one placeholder per key, so on the public route it grouped nothing (QA-184-s1-r3-01). It also read a component's cache declaration off the canonical spelling's symbol alone (CDX-184-r2-02). The runtime authority is revised to the component each reference binds to at apply: the builder's declared binding (`declared_bindings_for_components`, from `_will_reuse_at_apply`), recorded on the symbol as `bound_component_id`, then the resolved id. One accessor, `component_identity`, answers every same-component question, and a cache's declarations are gathered across all its references (`canonical_cache_profiles`). Sibling sweep over every `component_id` comparison in validation and effect resolution. Moved to the accessor: the canonical cache spelling, profile identity in connector resolution and in the bound-path rule, and the effect resolver's alias binding, duplicate-binding claim and caller cache seeds. Unchanged because they compare EMITTED or account-observed ids: lowering, the emitter registry's requirement index, the invariant id set, the entry operation id, and replay-grant corroboration. Every production symbol-table construction passes the request's policy (`test_every_production_symbol_table_names_its_conflict_policy`, derived from the source). Witnesses: the QA round's own probes through `plan_authoring_request_v1` (`test_two_specs_binding_one_cache_are_one_cache_on_the_typed_plan_route`), the profile sibling (`test_a_profile_is_compared_by_the_component_its_references_bind`) and the revision oracle `component_identity`. Coverage: every binding the request declares by id — reuse, `reference_only` and update — under each policy; a name-only binding needs an account read (C24). A declared id spelled like one of the plan's own placeholder ids binds nothing, so the accessor never equates an account id with a placeholder (SELF-184-04, `test_a_declared_id_spelled_like_a_plan_placeholder_binds_nothing`). **Revision 2 (correction batch 4):** batch 3 unified identity but still let ONE reference's spec speak for the component in two places. Effect derivation took the first alias in sort order, a regression batch 3 introduced (CDX-184-r3-01). Each reference also kept its own profile facts, so a `reference_only` alias of a map the request updates carried none. The authority is now the config apply writes into the component. `_written_spec` selects it for effect derivation through the plan's own `_may_be_substituted` authority. `_bound_component_facts` projects its facts onto every bound reference, and projects none when nothing in the request writes the component or two writes disagree. Sibling sweep over every place a component fact is read through one reference: symbol profile facts (input, output and cache) are projected; the cache declaration merge stays for hand-built tables; the effect resolver's cache and subprocess paths select spellings and child roots, not configs, and a canonical process root carries no declared binding, so its alias set is its own ref; connector metadata for a reused component comes from the resolution snapshot's live reading (#155), a separate authority. Witnesses: `test_a_map_declaration_derives_from_the_config_apply_writes` (both sort orders, both policies), `test_two_writes_of_one_map_that_disagree_decide_nothing`, `test_every_reference_to_a_bound_component_carries_the_written_facts`, and the `component_identity` oracle's `map_through_the_reference` and `declared_map_effect_through_its_writer` roots. **Revision 3 (correction batch 5):** batch 4 erased facts on disagreement. It projected one set of facts onto every bound reference, written specs included, and made an effect declaration inert whenever written configs differed at all. That dropped constraints the base enforced (CDX-184-r4-01, CDX-184-r4-03) and read obligations (CDX-184-r4-02). The rule is now monotone. A spec apply writes keeps its own facts, so every check that reads them still sees a disagreement; only a reference apply does not write takes the facts every written config agrees on. Map effects are derived per written spec: agreeing effects decide, whatever else the configs differ in, and disagreeing effects refuse the declaration as `content-mismatch` instead of making it inert. Sibling sweep: revision 2's two sites are the only places that project a fact or select a written config. Witnesses: `test_two_updates_of_one_cache_that_disagree_check_writes_against_both`, `test_two_writes_with_the_same_map_effect_keep_the_declaration`, `test_two_writes_of_one_map_with_different_effects_refuse_the_declaration`, `test_a_metadata_only_update_alias_keeps_the_structured_update_facts`, and the `component_identity` oracle's `write_against_two_update_declarations` root and `ambiguous_declaration` resolution. **Revision 4 (correction batch 6):** revision 3 still described a component two specs write. It admitted a flow that feeds the first of two map updates the profile the other write replaces (CDX-184-r5-01), and its per-write effect comparison read cache keys in the spelling each join named (CDX-184-r5-02). Four revisions chose between writes, and each choice was wrong in a new corner. The materially different action is to make the case unwritable instead of modelling it: a request in which two specs write one existing component is refused before anything is described (plan correction C25). Every other reference to a bound component then carries the one write's facts, or none when nothing in the request writes it, and a declared effect is compared by component: map, script and subprocess declarations each reach their comparison through the canonical spelling `canonical_cache_refs` derives. Sibling sweep: the two sites that select a written config (`_bound_component_facts` and `_written_map_effect`) now see at most one write; the three declared-effect comparisons are the only places an effect is compared by value, and all three are canonical; connector metadata still comes from the resolution snapshot (revision 2). Witnesses: `test_two_updates_of_one_cache_are_refused_on_every_route`, `test_two_writes_of_one_map_refuse_the_request`, `test_a_declared_cache_read_is_compared_by_component_not_spelling`, `test_every_declared_effect_is_compared_in_canonical_cache_spelling`, and the `component_identity` oracle's `write_conflict` verdict and `joined_declaration` resolution. Pre-commit verification of the batch found one sibling the sweep missed (SELF-184-08): the resolver derived a child's subprocess summary against an empty symbol table, so a child's cache aliases were two caches; the summary is now derived against the symbols the child is compiled with (`test_a_child_summary_keys_its_caches_by_component`). Coverage: every branch apply takes for a spec naming an existing id, pairwise, under each conflict policy, derived by running apply offline (`test_a_write_conflict_is_exactly_two_writes_of_one_existing_component`, `test_the_write_predicates_agree_with_what_apply_does`). **Revision 5 (correction batch 7, QA-184-s1-r7-02):** revisions 2 to 4 carried a bound reference's PROFILE facts from the writing spec but read every other symbol fact from the reference's own spec. A call through a metadata-only alias of an operation therefore had no action and was refused. The rule is now whole: the writing spec is the source of every symbol fact of each reference apply does not write, and `_bound_component_facts` is replaced by one named source selection, `_fact_source`. Sibling sweep over every field the symbol model carries: the connector family and action, the input, output and cache profiles, the listener input type and request profile, and the path-binding requirement are read from the source. The connection is a fact of the step, not of the component, so a reference naming one keeps its own (SELF-184-12); `ref`, the placeholder `component_id` and `bound_component_id` identify the reference and its component. Witness and coverage: `test_a_reference_apply_does_not_write_carries_every_fact_of_the_writing_spec` compares every field of the model, so a field added later is covered, and the `component_identity` oracle's write matrix now records connector facts and a GUID-spelling verdict. **Revision 6 (correction batch 8, CDX-184-r7-01):** revision 5 read every component fact from the writing spec, including facts its config does not state. A spec that only renames an operation states no action, so an alias's own declared action was replaced by nothing. A writer describes its component only in what its config states: each fact of a reference apply does not write is the writer's stated value, else the reference's own (`_overlay`). That keeps the evidence the request had before batch 7 for anything the writer does not restate. Sibling sweep: the connector family and action, the listener input type and request profile, the three profile facts and the path-binding requirement all pass through the overlay; the connection keeps its reverse rule (the reference's own first). Witness: `test_a_writer_that_states_no_fact_leaves_a_reference_its_own`. Coverage: `test_each_reference_fact_is_the_writers_stated_fact_else_its_own` checks every symbol-model field against the writer and the reference each measured alone; the oracle's `fact_sources` verdict adds a rename-only writer, and `_overlay` is perturbed in the revision test. **Revision 7 (correction batch 9), structural — the property is made unwritable:** revisions 2 to 6 each decided which spec's configuration describes a component that more than one spec names. Each rule was wrong in a new corner: the writer's unstated fields (CDX-184-r7-01), a step's connection (SELF-184-12), a derived default (QA-184-s1-r9-01, CDX-184-r8-01), a raw-XML replacement (CDX-184-r8-02) and a cleared field (CDX-184-r8-03). The materially different action is to stop answering the question. A component the request writes may be named by that one spec only (plan correction C25, revised), so no fact is projected between specs, and `_fact_source` and `_overlay` are deleted. Identity unification stays for specs naming a component nothing in the request writes, which each describe the component as the account stores it. Sibling sweep: the six fact reads in `build_symbol_table` (connector family and action, connection, listener facts, the profile facts, the path-binding requirement) were the only projection sites, and each now reads the spec itself; `_written_map_effect` still chooses among aliases, but at most one of them now writes. Witness: `test_the_round_r8_shapes_are_refused_before_any_fact_is_read`, which fails on `ddfc88f` with the other new refusal tests (81 failing cases, measured from a snapshot of that tree). Coverage: `test_a_write_conflict_is_a_written_component_named_twice` (every branch apply takes, pairwise, per policy, derived by running apply) and `test_every_symbol_is_described_by_its_own_spec` (every symbol-model field equals that spec alone, for specs naming a component nothing writes; it passes on `ddfc88f` too, because it pins what batch 9 keeps) |

Structural fix delivered in correction batch 18: one derivation shared by every route that compiles a canonical root, enforced by #180's source-derived sweep with the stale exemption removed. |
| `child-requirement-not-inherited-at-call` | a child-contract field read off directly authored nodes (bound paths, cache consumers) instead of off the obligations the walk discharges at each of the process's own calls | the lineage walk's per-call discharge of a child's entry contract | CDX-184-r1-03, CDX-184-r1-04, ARCH-184-r1-04 — **recurred after the structural fix** | second instance within one round, so the structural fix is in the same batch: an obligation this process cannot meet at a call is recorded as its own. Cache requirements are recorded at the discharge. Writer requirements are derived from refusals at the call's `/process_ref` that clear under the seeded writer. Sibling sweep over every contract field: document requirements are recorded through `_requires` in the call's stream step; required reads through `_classify_unmet_read`; mutated state and state knowledge are read from the child's contract in the derivation; writers and cache requirements are fixed here. Witnesses: `test_a_forwarding_call_passes_its_childs_cache_requirement_to_its_caller`, `test_a_forwarding_passthrough_passes_its_childs_bound_path_writer_to_its_caller` **Recurrence (architect evaluation 1, ARCH-184-r1-04):** the sweep's claim that document requirements are recorded through the one recorder was false for a child with no contract or an unknown entry form, and for a waited passthrough child's requirement with no profile, which the call skipped. Materially different action delivered in correction batch 18: a forwarding-transparency invariant derived from the entry contract's requirement-side fields, with a non-default case per field, so a field added later fails until it is forwarded. |

As the second instance, the fix is structural and lands in the same batch. The child contract carries `cache_writes_known`, derived from the only cache writers: Add to Cache and Remove from Cache steps, plus calls whose children's cache writes are themselves known. Every consumer of a call's cache writes reads that flag: `_caches_a_call_may_write` and the derivation's composition. Sibling sweep: rule 8 keeps `state_known`, because a map may change a process property the shared requirement reads; the retrieve overlay reads the cohorts this marking sets; no other site asks which caches a call writes. Witnesses: `test_a_call_whose_unknown_effects_are_a_map_writes_no_unlisted_cache` (with a hiding grandchild control) and the `child_forwarding` oracle's `a_mapping_call_before_a_reader`. Coverage: every node kind that writes a cache in this IR, `cache_put`, `cache_remove` and `process_call` |

| `revision-oracle-omits-changed-behaviour` | compiler-revision behaviour oracles whose hand-picked scenarios exercise no alias, effect declaration or forwarding call | the validation behaviour those rules implement | CDX-184-r2-04, SELF-184-07, SELF-184-11, SELF-184-14, SELF-184-16, SELF-184-28, CDX-184-r11-01 — **recurred after the structural fix**; ARCH-184-r1-09 — **recurred after the batch-12 structural fix**; SELF-184-46, SELF-184-54 — carried the class unlisted until correction batch 21 reconciled this column; CDX-184-r20-02, SELF-184-65, SELF-184-66 — **recurred after the batch-18 structural fix and after batch 19's two in-batch instance patches** | first instance; two oracle rows project the changed behaviour (`component_identity`, `child_forwarding`), with one perturbation witness per rule. **Second instance (correction batch 6, SELF-184-07), structural:** the `component_identity` row's write verdict was one hand-picked pair of cache updates, so the connector bind predicate that now decides a refusal moved no verdict. The row now projects the write predicate over its whole case set: for every spec kind a request can name an existing id with (a create by id, a `reference_only` create, an update, a `reference_only` update, and, for both connector families, a connector update authoring only metadata, one that only renames, which the smart merge writes, and one authoring a body) under each conflict policy, whether apply writes its config and whether it writes the existing component, and the symbol table's verdict for every pair of one component family. Sibling sweep: every predicate the batch introduced that decides plan-time behaviour is perturbed in `test_the_revision_moves_with_component_identity_and_forwarding_behaviour` (`component_write_conflicts`, `component_writes_existing`, `apply_writes_component_config`, `_binds_as_metadata_only_connector_update`, `smart_merge_would_change`, `_canonical_effect`, `_bound_component_facts`, `_written_map_effect`), and each perturbation must leave the row computable, so none moves the revision by breaking the oracle. A second verification of the batch found the renaming update missing from the case set (SELF-184-11): dropping the smart-merge half of the bind predicate flipped apply and the refusal and moved no verdict; it is added for both connector families. **Correction batch 7 (SELF-184-14, SELF-184-16):** the write matrix projected only the profile and connector facts of hand-built pairs, and the fact-source selection sat inline where nothing could perturb it, so reverting the reference_only binding's canonical id or the connector facts moved no row. The row now also projects every symbol-model field of each reference in one request that names an operation and a WSS listener through a metadata-only alias and reference_only specs spelling their GUIDs in upper case, one of them naming its own connection. `_fact_source` and `canonical_component_id` are perturbed in the revision test. The path-binding requirement needs an account snapshot the offline oracle does not build, and `test_a_reference_apply_does_not_write_carries_every_fact_of_the_writing_spec` witnesses it. **Correction batch 10 (SELF-184-28):** the oracle built every table without an account, so the binding a route resolves with one moved no verdict. The row gains `named_bindings`, a binding map standing for the account's answer: beside a writer it decides the write conflict, and without one it decides one bound component whose name-reused create contributes no profile fact. The revision test perturbs `_bound_existing_id` to ignore the map, which moves the revision (measured). **Correction batch 12 (CDX-184-r11-01), structural, the enumeration made derived:** each batch that added an identity reading to the typed plan's route left it out of the hand-listed oracle and perturbations (`planned_existing_ids` in batch 10, the batch-11 predicate that batch 12 then deletes), so the served compiler revision stayed identical while acceptance changed. The set is now read from the source: every function the validation route imports from the builder (`build_symbol_table`, `_validate_processes`, `plan_authoring_request_v1`), except the error class and the account scope name, which decide no verdict. Each must be reached by the oracle, directly or through `build_symbol_table`, and perturbed in the revision test, whose assertion is that every perturbation moves the revision. The oracle reads its account map through `planned_existing_ids`. Sibling sweep: of the identity functions measured, `planned_existing_ids` and the withdrawn predicate were the only ones whose perturbation moved no revision. Witness and coverage: `test_every_identity_decision_of_the_plan_route_moves_the_compiler_revision` **Recurrence (architect evaluation 1, ARCH-184-r1-09):** batch 12 derived the covered set from the typed plan route's builder imports, so no lineage decision was inside it: perturbing the retrieve overlay moved no revision. Materially different action delivered in correction batch 18: the set is derived from every module-level decision the lineage walk reaches and from the child-contract derivation, each with a perturbation that moves the revision. **Recurrence (Codex commit review r20, CDX-184-r20-02), answered by a materially different invariant and NOT by another oracle scenario:** batch 18 derived the covered set from every module-level decision the lineage walk reaches, so a carry INSIDE the walk's closures sat in no scenario and no decision unit — the ride-on carry across a stream replacement changed compile acceptance while all three served revisions stayed byte-identical, and `compare_capability_revision` and `compare_live_build_provenance` reported match for bindings minted before the change (apply is protected, because it recompiles first). One more oracle chain was measured and REJECTED as the disposition: it does rotate both revisions and does close the cell, but it is this class's own mechanism a fourth time, after three structural fixes (correction batches 6, 12 and 18) and two in-batch instance patches (SELF-184-54), which the CONTINUE test forbids. Correction batch 21b makes the compiler's own test corpus revision material instead: `compiler_revision` folds in the replayed verdicts of every graph the covered tests exercise, from a deduplicated corpus packaged with the server and replayed at runtime through the four entry points, so this class's mechanism — a behaviour change a test pins while the revision stands still — is unwritable anywhere in the compiler rather than only at module-level decisions. It is behaviour-derived and not a source hash, so identical packages replay identical corpora into identical revisions. The covered set is checkable rather than described: an in-suite guard harvests the same inputs during the run and refuses a covered test whose input the packaged corpus lacks. Each verdict is read through each class's own authority — fields, computed fields, named-tuple fields, slots, package-declared readable properties and containers tagged with their type — after the batch's own verification measured a hand-enumeration surviving three levels further out: finding fields, then the verdict containers, then a validation report's own fields, then container type and a plain readable property. Sibling sweep, measured: of 44 carry mutants in the lineage walk the batch-18 guard moves the revision for 16 and the corpus digest moves for 21 — every revision-silent carry a covered test kills, including the batch-18 ride-on attribution at the retrieve and the map-stream and caller-entry carries, silent since they were introduced. Non-vacuity: the batch-20 stream wrapper moves the corpus digest and the exact revert moves it back. The bound is served rather than implied — `docs/architecture/AUTHORING_WORKFLOW_V1.md` §4 states it in four parts — and §11's closure sentence is corrected to say that the chosen case sets were what left the revision standing still. |
| `reuse-decided-off-the-apply-predicate` | whether a component's config is materialized, read from `component_materialization_mode`, which knows `reference_only` but not the conflict policy | the builder's `_will_reuse_at_apply` predicate | SELF-184-01, SELF-184-05, SELF-184-23 | first instance in this slice. **Second instance (correction batch 6, SELF-184-05), structural:** the write-conflict check and profile facts read the `reference_only` flag, while apply writes a `reference_only` update: its planner turns only a `reference_only` create into a reuse, and its apply loop reads the flag only for a create. The authority is revised to apply's branches as a whole, read by one predicate, `apply_writes_component_config`: a `reference_only` create binds, `_will_reuse_at_apply` reuses a create naming an id, a connector update authoring only metadata binds, and everything else is written. Sibling sweep over every consumer of `component_materialization_mode`: profile facts and map-effect substitution now ask the predicate. Governance's reuse and ownership questions (which names and folders a request claims), the recipe slot contract, and deployment's fallback for a build with no result row ask the declared binding vocabulary, not whether apply writes a config, and are unchanged. Witness: `test_a_reference_only_update_is_described_by_its_own_config`. Coverage derived from the authority: every branch apply takes for a spec naming an existing id, measured by running apply offline per kind and policy (`test_the_write_predicates_agree_with_what_apply_does`, with `test_the_apply_outcomes_cover_every_branch` for non-vacuity). **Correction batch 10 (SELF-184-23):** the predicate answered a create reused by NAME as written, because its binding input was declared-only by the recorded decision. Once identity took the account's binding, that decision let a discarded configuration describe the shared component. The predicate keeps its branches and now reads the route's binding (`_bound_existing_id`), the same input the reuse set reads, so a create reused by name is discarded exactly as one reused by id; `test_a_binding_the_account_answers_decides_identity_conflicts_and_the_written_configuration` |
| `declared-binding-id-unnormalized` | a declared component id compared as authored on the create and update binding paths, while the `reference_only` path strips it | the planner binding (`resolve_planner_binding`) | SELF-184-02, QA-184-s1-r7-01, SELF-184-17, QA-184-s1-r10-01, SELF-184-19, SELF-184-20, SELF-184-21, SELF-184-22, SELF-184-24, SELF-184-25 — **recurred after the structural fix** | first instance; `declared_bindings_for_components` compares the stripped id. **Second instance (correction batch 7, QA-184-s1-r7-01), structural:** stripping answered one spelling difference at one comparison. The platform, meanwhile, reads a component GUID in any case but refuses an update sent through a URL id whose case differs from the component's own. The authority is revised to the platform's canonical spelling of a component id, read by one function, `canonical_component_id`: whitespace stripped, a blank value names nothing, a GUID lowercase. The planner binding returns it, so the write-conflict check, the declared bindings, the reuse set and every plan step read it. Sibling sweep over every declared-id use in the builder, read from its source: the planner binding's two branches, the plan's update-target check, the apply target, and a canonical root's plan and execute targets now read it; the one remaining use echoes the authored value into the served plan step. Whether a value is a component id is the replay registry's one id language (`connector_replay.ids`), which the function asks rather than restates. Pre-commit verification found one sibling outside the builder (SELF-184-17): governance compared a connection binding's two ids stripped, and now compares them through the same function. This also removes the blank-id divergence SELF-184-09 recorded, because apply's target now treats a blank top-level id as naming nothing, as the plan does. Witness: `test_ids_differing_only_in_guid_case_name_one_component`. Coverage: `test_every_declared_component_id_reaches_the_builder_through_the_canonical_authority` enumerates every use from the source and fails on a new raw one. **Recurrence (QA round r10, QA-184-s1-r10-01), mechanism revised:** the #184 identity readings (the declared bindings, the write-conflict check, whether apply writes an existing component) ask the planner binding in its declared-only form, which answers a declared id and never the account's exact-name match. A `create` the policy reuses by name, or a `reference_only` spec carrying only a name, therefore binds nothing to the check, while apply binds it to the component another spec writes. The canonical spelling fixed how a declared id is read; it could not supply a binding the declared reading does not have. The materially different action, owed to correction batch 10, is to hand the planner bindings that the route resolves with its account to every one of those readings. The typed plan and compile already hold the client, and apply resolves every spec with it in `_build_plan`. Only routes whose contract touches no account (the recipe engine, archetype composition) keep the declared reading, and a request from them is refused again at apply before any write. **Structural fix (correction batch 10):** every identity consumer asks one binding reading, `_bound_existing_id`, which answers which existing component a spec names: the reuse set, the declared bindings that unify symbols, whether an update writes an existing component, the write-conflict check, and whether apply writes a spec's configuration. A key the route resolved with its account is answered from that resolution, including an answer that binds nothing, and any other key from the declared reading. The resolution is ONE per route: the component plan apply builds (`_build_plan`), read by `planned_existing_ids`. Apply hands it to `_build_canonical_symbols`, `_build_canonical_plan` and `_execute_canonical_process` as a required argument, like `resolution`. The typed plan and compile run the same component plan once, before validation (it is the plan their component-plan lint already ran, so no read is added), and hand its bindings to validation. A first version bounded a separate plan-side reader instead; its pre-commit verification confirmed that the reader was itself a second model of apply's binding. It missed apply's name and type normalization and the generic wrapper, and it read nothing for a request without a writer (SELF-184-19..22, SELF-184-24, SELF-184-25), so it is deleted. Sibling sweep, read from the source: every call of an identity reading passes the route's bindings, except in the three modules that hold none (the recipe engine and the revision oracle touch no account, and effect derivation already answers "may be substituted" for a create under `reuse`); the declared-only binding is read for its id by `_bound_existing_id` alone. Witness: `test_plan_compile_and_apply_judge_one_binding`. Coverage: `test_every_identity_reading_takes_the_bindings_its_route_resolved` enumerates every call from the source |
| `served-diagnostic-narrower-than-its-refusals` | a hand-written served message, remediation and taxonomy summary for a diagnostic code, left describing its first reporting site after the code gained others | the sites that report the code: connector resolution's profile well-formedness and error-body continuity checks, and the lineage stream-profile proof at a map, a cache write, a profile source and a call into a passthrough child | QA-184-s1-r5-01, QA-184-s1-r6-01 | first instance; the served texts now name every reporting site, pinned in both directions to the sites read from the two modules' source (`test_the_served_profile_mismatch_text_names_every_reporting_site`). **Second instance (correction batch 6, QA-184-s1-r6-01), structural:** the pin joined the message, the remediation and the taxonomy summary into one string, so a site only the remediation named passed for a message that omitted it, and the served diagnostic summary IS that message. The invariant is now per served text: every reporting site read from the reporting modules' source is named by the message, by the remediation and by the taxonomy summary, each on its own (the joined form passed on `e2751a9`, whose message omitted an operation's profile reference). Sibling sweep over the served texts of the codes this slice added or extended: the entry-context code's taxonomy summary names both of its reporting sites (a Data Passthrough child called without waiting, refused by lineage; a passthrough process run directly, refused by deployment orchestration); the write-conflict code has one reporting site, symbol-table construction, which its summary names; the compiler architecture document names all five profile-mismatch sites across its well-formedness and continuity rules |

| `unanswered-component-plan-read-as-declared` | the typed plan's component plan read through the lint's exception-swallowing fallback, which answers any failure as no plan, so an unreadable account became the declared reading of component identity | the planner binding's contract that a failed metadata read is not an empty answer (`resolve_planner_binding`) | QA-184-s1-r11-01, QA-184-s1-r12-01, QA-184-s1-r13-01 — **recurred after the fix** | first instance; fixed in correction batch 11: with a client in hand, a component plan that could not be built blocks the plan wherever component identity waits on its answer (`component_identity_waits_on_the_account`), instead of degrading its bindings. Sibling sweep over the account readings that feed a gate on the typed plan: live readings mark a failed fetch `read_failed`, and a reused key read that way is refused in the snapshot; the reuse set for maps and profiles degrades to "written", the direction in which a check still runs; the served `resolved_references` is an echo, not a gate. The component plan was the only reading whose failure removed a refusal. **Second instance (QA round r12, QA-184-s1-r12-01), resolved by the owner decision of 2026-09-14:** batch 11's rule enumerated where identity waits on the unbuilt plan. The plan also decides refusals that hang on one spec (an ambiguous match, a collision under `fail`, the profile facts of a create it reuses by name), and those passed through it. A complete fail-closed rule blocks every request whose names the account could match, and that includes the clean-room suite's account-free compile. Its fixtures were never buildable component plans: each of their components fails the component-plan lint once the account answers (measured). The owner decided that an unreadable account degrades the typed plan instead of blocking it. What remains is derived from the authority's own guard. Apply compiles again against the account and refuses a binding the account decides differently, and a degraded plan is never presented as judged: its advisory names every verdict the component plan decides. The predicate `component_identity_waits_on_the_account` is deleted. Sibling sweep: the public boundary always builds a client (QA round r12), so the no-client advisory is reached only by unit tests. Witness: `test_an_unbuildable_component_plan_is_served_as_unjudged`. **Third instance (QA round r13, QA-184-s1-r13-01):** the owner decision degrades plan and compile, and a degraded plan must never be read as judged. Apply's own guard reads it exactly that way. Its recompile inside `preflight_typed_apply_v1` builds the component plan from the account, and when that fails it reproduces the degraded hash, so the binding check passes while the write loop's second read binds from the recovered account. The invariant is enforced where the plan is served, but not at the one consumer that certifies a binding for a write. Fixed in correction batch 13: the plan's internal result records whether an account was in hand and the component plan could not be built (`component_plan_unjudged`), and `preflight_typed_apply_v1`, the one consumer that confirms a binding for a write, refuses on it before comparing any binding. Sibling sweep: the raw apply builds its component plan with `_build_plan`, which raises before any write rather than degrading; plan and compile degrade by the owner decision; the recipe engine and archetype composition hold no account. Witness: `test_apply_refuses_a_binding_it_could_not_confirm_against_the_account` |

### Sibling inventory — `cache-step-document-emission` (amendment 3 §12; completed by correction batch 14)

The authority is the document-emission table in `src/boomi_mcp/models/process_ir_document_semantics.py` and its
derived sets. Its measured rows are bound to archived captures by the capture index in
`docs/architecture/evidence/issue-184/document_emission/`. Coverage is claimed over the
discovered finite compiler vocabulary (authored, semantic and emitter kinds, each derived by the test module from
its own source) and the indexed evidence cases, not every platform operation. Every witness below was collected
at correction batch 14.

Correction batch 11 makes the plan block (`AUTHORING_COMPILE_BLOCKED`, subject `component_plan`) when a client is in hand, its component plan could not be built, and component identity waits on that plan's answer, so compile issues no binding for such a request. Identity waits on it when a spec that only an account read can bind shares its metadata type with a spec bound by a declared id, or shares its type and name with another such spec. The rule is read from the request through apply's own normalization, metadata type and reuse predicate. Its first version blocked every plan whose component plan failed, and broke the account-free compile the clean-room suite serves; distinct names can never share a component, so that request compiles unchanged. Without a client the lint's advisory is unchanged; `test_an_unbuildable_component_plan_blocks_only_an_identity_the_account_decides`, which fails on `a117a03` (measured) |

| SELF-184-38 | correction batch 16's pre-commit verification (main thread, with the implementing agent measuring through `validate_body_capabilities`) | "`PROCESS_IR_SEMANTIC_CONTROL_CONTINUATION_UNSUPPORTED` serves, at the compiler layer, a remediation about steps after a branch or decision, although a lone connector-scoped handler ending in continue raises the same code, so that refusal is told to move steps into every leg or arm" | none (self) | machine-served schemas/contracts | `remediation-describes-one-raiser` | Standard — anchor: no source label; a served remediation is machine-served contract text, a blocking class | pre-existing at `73b9d3d`, measured through `validate_body_capabilities`: a lone connector-scoped handler ending in continue served the branch/decision remediation there, unchanged at `8048836` | fixed — the structural fix of the class's second instance: a code the compiler serves by translating a shared model rule serves the parser's remediation, one text at both layers, as the repo's rule for identical facts states; the continuation code now carries the parser's text, which describes both rules, plus its gate. `test_a_translated_code_serves_the_parsers_remediation` derives the translated-code set from the compiler and requires equal texts outside a pinned exemption set, and `test_a_continuation_refusal_serves_one_remediation_at_both_layers` serves the handler refusal at both layers. Of the 10 codes the compiler serves by translating a model rule, four carried different texts at the two layers (the branch leg-count and list-bound cardinality codes, the error-scope code and `PROCESS_IR_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED`); each now serves one text, which for the latter two also covers the compiler-only rules that raise them, and the exemption set is empty. The sibling sweep also points the cardinality text at the node kind's own page for root-sequence rules, since the placement category carries control-body slots only. It records that the try/catch-successor rule is spelled under the error-scope code only by a direct `validate_body_capabilities` call, because both public entry points re-parse first and serve the continuation code. Changing one compiler text of a translated code fails the parity invariant (measured). What is checked mechanically is the gate names and layer parity; whether a remedy's wording fits every raising rule beyond that is prose and is not decided by a guard |

Its reason, keeping the #154 sentence that an undeclared effect never establishes state, does not hold for a server-derived contract: the derivation already computes the must-set a declaration would be checked against, and `cap184-dpp-both-ways` attests a waited, abort-on-error child's process property reaching a later parent leg. Rule 8 asks for analysis over the lattice until stable: the set-overlap refusal over-reaches for process properties, whose establishment only grows, and is right for caches, because the lattice cannot express a possible removal (a child that reads a cache untyped and removes it stays refused). A qualifier the fix honours: the exit meet left out a terminal call, so a child with one arm ending in a call over-claimed its must-set (measured) (C20a) The contract carries the execution state the child establishes on every normal completion, gated at the call by wait, abort-on-error and a path that provably runs it; a whole-cache removal un-establishes the cache wherever the walk proves one; and the repeated-invocation rule refuses only the caches the child requires and may write. | WAVE-184-r1-01 | composite wave gate (loop 4), evaluation 1: `scripts/wave_gate.py wave --base 73b9d3d --require-plan-fingerprint` at `80bdd30` (clean), run to measure currency before §6; it refused at the node-manifest check, before the suite (archived `docs/architecture/evidence/issue-184/wave-gate/wave-184-r1.80bdd30/`) | "rows [204 ids] are appended already tombstoned; a tombstone records the retirement of a row that existed, so an identity that was never active cannot be introduced retired. A row added and removed within the same range needs no row at all" (the gate's manifest-transition refusal; its code name is omitted for the ledger token scanner) | none (gate refusal) | none — pytest-node manifest bookkeeping, nothing served | none | Non-blocking — anchor: gate bookkeeping, nothing served; corrected because the wave gate refuses the tree without it | `80bdd30` | fixed — per-batch reconciliation appended rows in one correction batch and retired them in a later one, which is legal per commit but, across the range the gate judges, a retirement of an identity that never existed at either endpoint Correction batch 18 drops the rows appended and retired inside the slice's range and renumbers the appended active rows, so the manifest is legal from the landing base. The repository's own successor guard refuses a deletion-plus-repoint, and it names this collision: the two rules meet head-on at the close, and the gate's base transition is the one that judges the landing. Its waiver admits exactly one regeneration, and this is that one — legal against the landing base, repairing a committed manifest that is not legal there, removing nothing outside the rows the landing base never had and HEAD carries as tombstones, and with the appended block re-derived in COLLECTION ORDER, ranked by a canonical collection (Python 3.11 with the pinned pytest, plugin autoload off, 13245 nodes). A first attempt kept the order the batches appended in and was refused for exactly that. Measured at `5b5038c`: both arms serve compiler `sha256:40def511…`, capability `sha256:194aa6c8…` and schema `sha256:c9583e6b…`, every `_compiler_revision_payload` row digest equal and no row reading unavailable, while the literal-in-the-re-cache-target cell past a Message flips from admitted at `4c67685` to refused at HEAD on validate, `compile_process_ir_v1` and `parse_and_compile_process_ir_v1`; neutralising the carry in memory at HEAD moves zero payload rows and makes all six cells byte-equal to the base arm's. Through the public dispatcher a plan and binding minted on the base arm get `status match` from `compare_capability_revision` and `revision_skew match` with no mismatches and nothing uncompared from `compare_live_build_provenance` at HEAD, which refuses the same request; apply is protected because it recompiles first (`AUTHORING_APPLY_VALIDATION_REQUIRED`), so the false match is confined to verify and the capability comparison, which is precisely what `compiler_revision` exists for. The instance patch was measured and rejected AS the disposition: adding one chain to the child-forwarding oracle does rotate the revisions (compiler `sha256:a353d008…`, capability `sha256:56b138f0…`) and does close this cell, but it is the class's own mechanism a fourth time and leaves the next in-closure carry silent. The invariant instead: `compiler_revision` now folds in the replayed verdicts of every graph the covered tests exercise, from a deduplicated corpus packaged with the server (`src/boomi_mcp/authoring/revision_corpus_v1.json.gz`, ≈183 KB gzipped) and replayed at runtime through `resolve_process_ir_effect_declarations`, `validate_process_ir`, `compile_process_ir_v1` and `parse_and_compile_process_ir_v1`, so the class's mechanism — a behaviour change a test pins while the revision stands still — is unwritable anywhere in the compiler, not only at module-level decisions. The corpus holds INPUTS only; every verdict is computed by the running code, and the corpus is part of the package, so identical packages replay identical corpora into identical revisions. An in-suite guard (`tests/_revision_corpus.py`, registered through `tests/conftest.py` and installed by a `sys.meta_path` hook before collection) harvests the same inputs during the run and refuses a covered test whose input the packaged corpus lacks, so the covered set is checkable rather than described. A verdict is read through each class's own authority — a model by its fields AND its computed fields, a named tuple by its fields, a slotted object by its slots, a readable package `property`, and a container item by item WITH its type — so a validation report's `is_valid` and `version`, a resolution's every slot and its `ok`, and a compile result's lowered CFG all ride, and a field added to any of them rides the day it exists. Sibling sweep, measured rather than argued: of 44 carry mutants in the lineage walk the previous guard moved the revision for 16 and the corpus digest moves for 21 — every revision-silent carry a covered test kills, including the batch-18 ride-on attribution at the retrieve, the map-stream origin and the caller-entry identity, all three of which had been silent since they were introduced. Non-vacuity: the batch-20 stream wrapper moves the corpus digest and the exact revert moves it back (`test_the_ride_on_carry_past_a_stream_replacement_moves_the_compiler_revision`, `test_a_carry_the_tests_kill_moves_the_compiler_revision`, `test_an_unchanged_walk_recompiled_moves_nothing`). The bound is served, not implied: `docs/architecture/AUTHORING_WORKFLOW_V1.md` §4 states it in four parts — a change no oracle case and no corpus input exhibits does not move it; only what an entry point RETURNS is folded in; only the outermost entry-point call of a covered test is recorded; and a verdict is everything the entry point returned, digested — and §11's stale closure sentence is corrected to say the chosen case sets were what left the revision standing still. Merged tree: compiler `sha256:08213c91abd1602bb122a904f44a385749dafbf797ccb0ead8ec4cd171f1aa44`, capability `sha256:555f0eb16a66f7c772eff40c9896af294a6bd8896cfca5b5245aa632f078e12a`, schema `sha256:c9583e6b971f3461f9dd6c78e0480cd5e2104c249089906154c0f93f4bd158dd` unchanged, with the #149 reachability inventory rebaselined through its own producer. Three limits are recorded inside the bound rather than closed, each measured: a module copy loaded from a hand-built spec never reaches `sys.meta_path`, so its entry points stay unwrapped and a novel call through it escapes the guard — no in-suite route reaches it, and the exclusion is named in the hook's own docstring; the short-circuit writes the literal `not compared` into the corpus row whenever another row already differs, so a converted pre-existing test's unavailable-scan cannot see a degraded corpus row in that branch — deliberate, and pinned both ways by `test_the_short_circuit_names_the_row_it_did_not_compute`; and one level below the memo's stated module surface `copy._copy_dispatch[dict]` is held by identity only and serves a stale row, named in the bound as the one measured live instance of 35 module-level containers swept (`test_the_bound_names_the_container_item_the_key_cannot_see`) |

served contract text is machine-facing API | `5b5038c`..the batch | fixed in correction batch 21, by splitting the code rather than widening a third text — the round measured the defect as an author receives it, through `build_integration` on the plan route and the compile route, which serve identical text: code `AUTHORING_COMPILE_BLOCKED`, cause `PROCESS_IR_CAPABILITY_PROCESS_CALL_PLACEMENT_UNSUPPORTED` at `/body/steps/1/legs/2/terminal`, a message about a call placed after a composition the IR does not admit, and a remediation whose first two sentences describe a step prefix that this call — already the terminal of an empty-prefix Branch leg — does not have. A needle search over the message, the remediation, the cited diagnostic entry and every page the pointers return scored zero hits for a cause and zero for an alternative; only `node.process_call` fact[0] states a rule, and it says a No Data child is refused when it may change a cache it reads FIRST, while this child writes the cache in one leg before retrieving it in another. Widening the placement text again is what the class's batch-6 fix already did once, so the batch takes the materially different action: rule 8 now raises its own served code, `PROCESS_IR_CAPABILITY_PROCESS_CALL_REPEATED_RUN_UNSTABLE`, and the placement code goes back to describing one rule. The new code's message states the cause — the called No Data process runs once for each document reaching the call, and each run may change state a later run requires, naming the two causes (reading the cache before writing it; using what it retrieves from a cache it may also add to or empty) — and its remediation names the ways out in the order an author can act on them: make the call run once for these documents by giving the child a Data Passthrough entry or calling it from a stepless Branch leg of a process with no explicit entry; or answer the cause, with the read-before-write cause and the retained-content cause given separate, non-interchangeable answers, including the whole published cleanup recipe and the declared-outside-writer condition that takes no exemption at all. | SELF-184-59 | correction batch 21a's pre-commit verification (main thread; six adversarial verification rounds over seven fix rounds, each finding reproduced by a verifier that did not write the code, with its probes archived under the batch's scratch record `b21a/` and `b21a_verify`..`b21a_verify6`), finding B21A-R2-FO-01 and W21A-01 / R8-CONFIRM-01, the two directions of one gap, confirmed pre-existing at `5b5038c` by the round-2 verifier | "The re-cache attribution gap in both directions: a property a process cannot prove, retrieved from one cache and re-cached into another, loses the cache it came from — so a child that binds on it is refused with a contract naming nothing any caller could satisfy, while a caller whose documents lack the property is admitted at the call although the flattened twin refuses it" | none (self) | runtime behavior; capability reachability | `re-cache-drops-the-origin-of-what-it-stored` — the origin cache of an unproved property dropped by the cohort a re-cache stores; runtime authority: the cohorts the lineage walk freezes at each cache write and the caches those documents came out of. First instance | Standard — anchor: no source label; one direction is a fail-open admission and the other refuses a runtime-correct composition, both in blocking classes | `5b5038c`..the batch | fixed with one carrier serving both directions — measured at both arms before the correction, and identical at each, so neither direction was this batch's doing. Over-refusal: the smallest shape, a child whose Branch re-caches without writing the property and then uses it, is refused `PROCESS_IR_SEMANTIC_LINEAGE_PROPERTY_READ_BEFORE_WRITE` or `PROCESS_IR_SEMANTIC_DYNAMIC_PATH_DDP_NOT_ESTABLISHED` for EVERY caller with an empty requirement list, while the flattened twin is clean for the caller that provably stores the property — a runtime-correct graph refused with nothing an author could act on; the controls that take the same use straight off the first retrieve are clean with the row recorded, so the machinery worked and the re-cache was the gap. Fail-open: the mirror shape, where the child empties the target cache, refills it from what it retrieved from the caller's other cache and binds on a property of what it retrieves, is admitted at the call with an empty contract while the twin is refused. One carrier closes both: the cohort a write stores now records the caches its documents came out of, so a later use off the re-cache becomes a requirement against the ORIGIN cache instead of an in-child refusal, and the caller that stored property-less documents there is charged. Measured: 16 matrix cells move from admitted to refused on the ordinary channel and 12 from refused to admitted where the removal is proved, counted per cell and caller with both entry points agreeing. Recorded limit, measured and deliberately not closed: the origin does not survive a connector call or a map between the retrieve and the re-cache, because both branches rebuild the stream — the count carry survives only because it is reapplied afterwards, and the origin carry is not. The direction is fail-CLOSED (the per-property caller-cache token survives a connector call, so a proved use still owes its caches; only the unproved-origin channel is lost), it is identical at `5b5038c`, and extending the carrier past a connector call would be a behaviour change, since the documents there are the connector's output and not the caller's. First instance and, at the seeded pass, **second instance** inside the batch, so the second correction is the invariant rather than another seed | Standard — anchor: no source label; a fail-open admission in a blocking class, pre-existing at `5b5038c` and defeating this batch's own new refusals | `5b5038c`..the batch | fixed, in two corrections whose second is structural — measured first as the decisive triple: an identical child, refused `PROCESS_IR_CAPABILITY_PROCESS_CALL_REPEATED_RUN_UNSTABLE` with its full row, becomes ADMITTED with every contract field empty when one extra decision-guarded leg introduces a back edge anywhere below it, because a root is ordered only when all its call targets already are, and neither a cycle member nor anything that transitively calls one ever is. An unordered root is now seeded with the contract an underivable call gets and derived once against those seeds, so its own obligations and entry form are real while everything the seeding hid stays unknown. The round-6 verifier then measured the residue that seeding left: with each unordered root derived once against a FROZEN seed map, an obligation BETWEEN two members reached nobody — a grandparent storing property-less documents, a member filling the cache and a partner binding on it validated and compiled clean at every root, on three back-edge spellings, while the acyclic twin and `5b5038c` both refused it. The second correction is the invariant the first lacked: the unordered set is derived to a FIXED POINT on what a member owes its callers — a set that only grows over a finite lattice, so it terminates — and the row travels, the caller being refused `PROCESS_IR_SEMANTIC_DYNAMIC_PATH_DDP_NOT_ESTABLISHED` plus `PROCESS_IR_CAPABILITY_PROCESS_CALL_REPEATED_RUN_UNSTABLE` on both entry points exactly as the acyclic twin's caller is, while a grandparent that stores the property keeps shipping. Measured across the cycle probes as 30 verdict entries moving admitted to refused and 6 refused to admitted, each of the six a member whose refusal became its caller's inherited row. Witnesses: `test_a_call_cycle_below_a_child_does_not_buy_it_the_repeat_exemption` (the decisive triple), `test_a_cycle_member_still_states_what_it_requires_of_its_callers`, `test_an_obligation_between_two_members_of_one_cycle_reaches_the_caller`; non-vacuity `test_the_cycle_seed_is_what_refuses_it` and `test_the_fixpoint_is_what_carries_the_obligation_out_of_the_cycle`, which reverts the fixpoint to the single pass and re-admits the request. Two recorded limits, both measured. First, the fixpoint carries what a member OWES and not what it may have DONE: a whole-cache removal is an EXISTENCE claim that ENDS a caller's obligation, so inheriting one from a partner whose facts were derived against seeds could weaken a caller's verdict rather than tighten it — deliberately outside the iterated set, so a whole-cache removal a cycle-reachable child inherits from its partner stays invisible to its caller and a caller that fills, calls and then reads is admitted where the acyclic twin is refused `PROCESS_IR_SEMANTIC_LINEAGE_CACHE_WRITER_MISSING`. It equals the answer an ABSENT ProcessIR gives, so the never-weaker-than-absent invariant holds exactly, and nothing moved in this batch. Second, a cycle-reachable No Data child that requires a cache is refused with a finding that does not mention the cycle: the refusal is right and fail-closed, but its served causes are the rule-8 ones and the way out an author could act on does not change the verdict while the cycle stands, whereas the one that does — break the cycle, or give the child a Data Passthrough entry — no surface says.
