The comment beside the bound said it was larger than the number of growth steps the lattice allows and that no parser-valid request could reach it; both were false" | none (self) | runtime behavior | `fixpoint-termination-argued-but-not-enforced` — the same fixed-point iteration, with its termination resting on a hand-typed pass count standing in for the lattice's height, an argument its code did not check; runtime authority: the carried lattice itself, as for CDX-184-r21-01. Second instance of the pair, so the structural-fix rule applies in this batch: the count is replaced by the invariant it stood in for | Standard — anchor: no source label; runtime behavior is a blocking class, and none of the critical anchors (secrets/security, data loss, mutation accounting) applies — the failure is a raise, never an admission | `260eb8c`..the batch | fixed in correction batch 22 by the structural fix — reproduced before it was fixed (`docs/plans/issue-184-resume/work/b22b/repro.py`, gitignored like every probe this row names): a self-relay over nine caches and a guarded passthrough self-relay over eight and over nine raised on both entry points, `plan_authoring_request_v1` raised on the nine-cache self-relay and on a two-member ring over eight (bound 15), and `build_integration` plan served the untyped `RuntimeError` envelope; after the fix each resolves to a derived contract, validates with no error, and the tool serves its ordinary typed answer. The verification found nothing that oscillates: every field of every row was constant or grew across the passes it measured. The INVARIANT: the pass counter is gone, and termination rests on monotone growth over a finite universe, checked on every pass from the second. Every field of `ChildEntryContractV1` except its key is classed once, read off the model itself: the fields where a larger value is fail-closed for a caller (the four obligation row sets, the positional `document_requirements`, and `unwaited_cache_writes`, which joins the carry here — its derivation was already a union of the callees' previous rows, so carrying it moves no row) are carried as a union, the positional one as its distinct entries; the fields where a larger value is PERMISSIVE (`removed_caches`, `guaranteed_state`, and `mutated_state`, which also caps what may be guaranteed) are taken from the latest derivation and never carried, as D5b requires, but ASSERTED to contain the previous pass's value; every scalar (the entry form and the four claims the seed pins) is asserted equal. A tuple-valued field no class names, or two classes name, is refused before the first pass. So from the second pass a row that changes has strictly gained an element, every element is built from what the request names, and the loop ends at the first pass that changes no row. A broken assertion is a code defect and raises naming the root, the field and both values — never answered with the unknown seed, which drops every derived obligation, nor with the last pass's rows, which under-approximate what a caller owes. It stays a `RuntimeError`, not the compiler's `PROCESS_IR_COMPILE_INTERNAL`: measured, the typed plan and compile routes catch neither around this resolver, so the compiler's code would reach a caller no more typed. SIBLING SWEEP over `src/boomi_mcp/authoring` and `src/boomi_mcp/compiler`: no other fixed-point loop rests on a hand-typed count — the root ordering in `process_ir_effects.py` is bounded by the roots, the contract module's cycle finder is a generator, the topology's dependency walk is Tarjan's stack, and the lineage walk's visit bound is derived from the node count, one visit per node of a tree. NON-VACUITY: `test_the_pass_counter_this_replaced_refused_every_witnessed_relay` injects the old counter back into the source and each witnessed relay raises again, while the longest relay the old counter still settled answers identically to the working source; `test_a_derivation_that_takes_a_fact_back_is_refused_naming_it` builds a derivation that takes back a removal, a guarantee, a mutation or the entry form on the second pass and asserts the refusal names it; `test_a_classification_that_misses_a_field_is_refused_before_the_first_pass` removes and doubles a field's class. Source mutants, each killed: dropping the per-pass check (five tests fail), weakening it to set growth only (the finite-representation witness fails, under a watchdog so a regression fails rather than hangs), dropping the classification check (two), and carrying `removed_caches` (three). COVERAGE, derived from the authority's full case set: `test_every_contract_field_moves_by_exactly_one_rule` checks the classification against every field of the model, and the relay witness `test_a_re_cache_relay_settles_however_many_caches_it_names` covers the growth patterns the verification found — a guarded passthrough self-relay over eight caches settling in 10 passes where the bound was 9, a self-relay over twenty in 22 (bound 9), a two-member ring over thirteen in 16 (bound 15) and a three-member ring over seven in 25 (bound 21), each asserting one cached-property row per cache, no validate or compile error on any root, identical answers on both entry points and the pass count the verification measured with the bound lifted. Blast radius, against the batch's first correction: the adversarial lens's 3,008 requests all settle, with `capabilities_by_root` and the derivation counts byte-identical; the sweep lens's 47,096 distinct graphs are identical; of 7,968 stress requests 1,136 raised before and every one now settles to the lifted-bound result, the other 6,832 unchanged; the 216-cell matrix and the 1,282 probe cells, 1,498 cells in all with the batch-21 and batch-22 cycle probes among them, move nothing. The pin comment, witnesses and sort key added after that leave every served row byte-identical over the same corpora, both entry points, the matrix and 1,318 probe cells. An adversarial check of the invariant then tried to reach the refusal from a parser-valid request — three lenses, one per field class, about 30,000 requests (`docs/plans/issue-184-resume/work/b22c/`, gitignored) — and none did: `removed_caches` is derived only from the root's own removals and its callees' rows, so it grows by induction; `guaranteed_state` and `mutated_state` are empty for every unordered root on every pass; no scalar moves after the first pass. It found the invariant's one unstated precondition: the guarantee rule itself is NOT monotone in a callee's removals — a waited call un-establishes every cache the callee's `removed_caches` names before its guarantee applies — and those two fields stay empty only because every unordered root calls a member pinned `state_known=False` and `cache_writes_known=False`; the re-applied retention pin is load-bearing the same way, its raw value flipping in 89,808 pass transitions. The loop's comment now states that dependency, and `test_the_pinned_claims_are_what_keep_a_cycle_members_guarantee_monotone` (two rings that settle, and that raise naming `guaranteed_state` once the pin is relaxed in memory) and `test_the_re_applied_pin_is_what_keeps_the_retention_claim_still` make it a failing test rather than a comment. The set carry's sort key ordered `None` as the empty string, so rows `(C, None)` and `(C, "")` would have tied and left set iteration to order them, a set-equal, tuple-unequal pair the check refuses; unreachable, since every profile-ref source refuses an empty ref, and now impossible, with every served row's order unchanged over the corpora. Cost, recorded: with no pass count the passes grow with the request — a ring of 40 members, one of them re-caching 23 caches, settles in 961 passes and 30.8 s at the resolver, where the old bound raised. Served material, on the batch's final tree: `compiler_revision` `sha256:46792b20c15e745771740d5a5355ea9af630b17f55035a776c8882447861a81f` and `capability_revision` `sha256:973f19858de3ec6ffff1ebd46d0d080d3c3e7d02bcda77039082834007918749`, `schema_revision` unchanged at `sha256:c9583e6b971f3461f9dd6c78e0480cd5e2104c249089906154c0f93f4bd158dd`. They move because the regenerated behaviour corpus grew from 6,515 to 6,820 inputs, 305 added and none dropped — 268 with the relay witnesses, eight of them resolutions the old code raised on, and 37 with the pin and seed witnesses; no record already in the corpus changed its verdict and none of the 27 non-corpus revision rows moved. No served text changed — the one new string is the internal refusal, unreachable for a parser-valid request; the #149 fixture carries the new revisions, and the §11 tables did not move. Measured from the run logs first: at the landing base (`dev` run `34678722359` at `cbab28f`, green) the pytest span was 38.3 minutes, 32.6 of them the pre-existing `tests/test_issue_149_legacy_reachability_freeze.py`; on this branch that module took about 38.4 minutes, and the stretch of the suite from 29% to 44% — the #184 modules — took 829 s against 89 s for that stretch at the base, with batch 21's guard module `tests/test_issue_184_revision_corpus.py` still running at the kill. (a) The guard module was sped up from 597 s to 305 s on 3.11 (about 272 s in suite order), its whole-corpus replays cut from 66 to 28, without dropping a node id. The saving has a stated cost: 17 witnesses now detect a move at the first changed verdict instead of comparing the full served revisions. The argument is that the corpus row is SHA-256 over the verdicts sorted by input digest, so one differing verdict means a different row and therefore a different served revision, SHA-256 collisions aside — the caveat every revision comparison already carries. It holds only while the hashing path is the real one, so that path is checked by identity and, if anything in it is perturbed, the witness falls back to computing and comparing the whole row; the moved verdict is then replayed with the perturbation undone and must equal the baseline's. The end-to-end served chain is still computed in full by the ride-on witness, `test_the_ride_on_carry_past_a_stream_replacement_moves_the_compiler_revision`, and restoring the literal comparisons would cost about 82 s. (b) With the owner's approval on 2026-09-21, `timeout-minutes` on the job was raised from 60 to 120, recorded with its measurements in a comment in `.github/workflows/tests.yml`. That is a measured budget decision, not a defect fix: the cap exists to kill a hung job, not to bound a healthy suite, and at the landing base the suite already spent 38.3 of its 60 minutes, 32.6 of them in one module that predates this slice |

## Evidence index

Collected run directories are archived under `docs/architecture/evidence/issue-184/` with
`index.jsonl` + `SHA256SUMS`, in the batch that collects them. The attestation scanner checks every
archive that has an `index.jsonl`: `SHA256SUMS` must cover exactly the on-disk archive (minus
itself) AND agree with the git index. So the skeleton created with this ledger covers the
pre-baseline `oracle/` capture and the `predicates/` measurements, and every later capture or
collected run is added to `SHA256SUMS` in the SAME commit that adds its files — an archive file
never sits uncommitted or unlisted across a validation boundary.
